class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Left-_and_right-hand_traffic" title="Left- and right-hand traffic">Driving side</a></th><td class="infobox-data">left</td></tr><tr class="mergedtoprow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Telephone numbers in the Falkland Islands">Calling code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/Telephone_numbers_in_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Telephone numbers in the Falkland Islands">+500</a></td></tr><tr class="mergedrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Postcodes_in_the_United_Kingdom#British_Overseas_Territories" title="Postcodes in the United Kingdom">UK postcode</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><div class="postal-code">FIQQ 1ZZ</div></td></tr><tr class="mergedbottomrow"><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166" title="ISO 3166">ISO 3166 code</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/ISO_3166-2:FK" title="ISO 3166-2:FK">FK</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><a href="/wiki/Country_code_top-level_domain" title="Country code top-level domain">Internet TLD</a></th><td class="infobox-data"><a href="/wiki/.fk" title=".fk">.fk</a></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Website</th><td class="infobox-data"><span class="url"><a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.falklands.gov.fk/">www<wbr />.falklands<wbr />.gov<wbr />.fk</a></span></td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>The <b>Falkland Islands</b> (<span class="rt-commentedText nowrap"><span class="IPA nopopups noexcerpt" lang="en-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/English" title="Help:IPA/English">/<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="/ɔː/: &#39;au&#39; in &#39;fraud&#39;">ɔː</span></span>(<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span></span>)<span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="/ə/: &#39;a&#39; in &#39;about&#39;">ə</span><span title="&#39;n&#39; in &#39;nigh&#39;">n</span><span title="&#39;d&#39; in &#39;dye&#39;">d</span></span>,<span class="wrap"> </span><span style="border-bottom:1px dotted"><span title="/ˈ/: primary stress follows">ˈ</span><span title="&#39;f&#39; in &#39;find&#39;">f</span><span title="/ɒ/: &#39;o&#39; in &#39;body&#39;">ɒ</span><span title="&#39;l&#39; in &#39;lie&#39;">l</span><span title="&#39;k&#39; in &#39;kind&#39;">k</span></span>-/</a></span></span>;<sup id="cite_ref-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-5">&#91;5&#93;</a></sup> Spanish: <i lang="es">Islas Malvinas</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="es-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Spanish" title="Help:IPA/Spanish">[ˈizlaz malˈβinas]</a></span>) is an <a href="/wiki/Archipelago" title="Archipelago">archipelago</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">South Atlantic Ocean</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Patagonian_Shelf" title="Patagonian Shelf">Patagonian Shelf</a>. The principal islands are about 300&#160;mi (480&#160;km) east of South America's southern <a href="/wiki/Patagonia" title="Patagonia">Patagonian</a> coast and about 752&#160;mi (1,210&#160;km) from <a href="/wiki/Cape_Dubouzet" title="Cape Dubouzet">Cape Dubouzet</a> at the northern tip of the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_Peninsula" title="Antarctic Peninsula">Antarctic Peninsula</a>, at a latitude of about 52°S. The archipelago, with an area of 4,700&#160;sq&#160;mi (12,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup>), comprises <a href="/wiki/East_Falkland" title="East Falkland">East Falkland</a>, <a href="/wiki/West_Falkland" title="West Falkland">West Falkland</a>, and 776 smaller islands. As a <a href="/wiki/British_overseas_territory" class="mw-redirect" title="British overseas territory">British overseas territory</a>, the Falklands have internal <a href="/wiki/Self-governance" title="Self-governance">self-governance</a>, but the United Kingdom takes responsibility for their defence and foreign affairs. The capital and largest settlement is <a href="/wiki/Stanley,_Falkland_Islands" title="Stanley, Falkland Islands">Stanley</a> on East Falkland.
</p><p>Controversy exists over the Falklands' discovery and subsequent colonisation by Europeans. At various times the islands have had French, British, Spanish, and Argentine settlements. Britain <a href="/wiki/Reassertion_of_British_sovereignty_over_the_Falkland_Islands_(1833)" title="Reassertion of British sovereignty over the Falkland Islands (1833)">reasserted its rule in 1833</a>, but Argentina maintains its claim to the islands. In April 1982 Argentine military forces <a href="/wiki/1982_invasion_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="1982 invasion of the Falkland Islands">invaded the islands</a>. British administration was restored two months later at the end of the <a href="/wiki/Falklands_War" title="Falklands War">Falklands War</a>. In a <a href="/wiki/2013_Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_referendum" title="2013 Falkland Islands sovereignty referendum">2013 sovereignty referendum</a> almost all Falklanders voted in favour of remaining a UK overseas territory. The territory's sovereignty status is part of an ongoing <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_dispute" title="Falkland Islands sovereignty dispute">dispute between Argentina and the UK</a>.
</p><p>The population (3,662 inhabitants in 2021)<sup id="cite_ref-auto2_1-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-1">&#91;1&#93;</a></sup> is primarily native-born <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islanders" title="Falkland Islanders">Falkland Islanders</a>, the majority of British descent. Other ethnicities include French, <a href="/wiki/Gibraltarians" title="Gibraltarians">Gibraltarians</a>, and Scandinavians. Immigration from the United Kingdom, the South Atlantic island of <a href="/wiki/Saint_Helena" title="Saint Helena">Saint Helena</a>, and Chile has reversed a population decline. The predominant (and official) language is English. Under the <a href="/wiki/British_Nationality_(Falkland_Islands)_Act_1983" title="British Nationality (Falkland Islands) Act 1983">British Nationality (Falkland Islands) Act 1983</a>, Falkland Islanders are <a href="/wiki/British_nationality_law" title="British nationality law">British citizens</a>.
</p><p>The islands lie at the boundary of the <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_climate" title="Oceanic climate">subantarctic oceanic</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tundra_climate" title="Tundra climate">tundra climate</a> zones, and both major islands have mountain ranges reaching 2,300&#160;ft (700&#160;m). They are home to large bird populations, although many no longer breed on the main islands due to predation by <a href="/wiki/Introduced_species" title="Introduced species">introduced species</a>. Major economic activities include fishing, tourism and sheep farming, with an emphasis on high-quality wool exports. Oil exploration, licensed by the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_Government" class="mw-redirect" title="Falkland Islands Government">Falkland Islands Government</a>, remains controversial as a result of maritime disputes with Argentina.
</p>
<meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" />
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_Falkland_Islands_placenames" title="List of Falkland Islands placenames">List of Falkland Islands placenames</a></div>
<p>The name "Falkland Islands" comes from <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Sound" title="Falkland Sound">Falkland Sound</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Strait" title="Strait">strait</a> that separates the two main islands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJones200973_6-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJones200973-6">&#91;6&#93;</a></sup> The name "Falkland" was applied to the channel by <a href="/wiki/John_Strong_(mariner)" title="John Strong (mariner)">John Strong</a>, captain of an English expedition that landed on the islands in 1690. Strong named the strait in honour of <a href="/wiki/Anthony_Cary,_5th_Viscount_Falkland" title="Anthony Cary, 5th Viscount Falkland">Anthony Cary, 5th Viscount Falkland</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Treasurer_of_the_Navy" title="Treasurer of the Navy">Treasurer of the Navy</a> who sponsored his journey.<sup id="cite_ref-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-7">&#91;7&#93;</a></sup> The Viscount's title originates from the town of <a href="/wiki/Falkland,_Fife" title="Falkland, Fife">Falkland</a>, Scotland—the town's name probably comes from a <a href="/wiki/Scottish_Gaelic" title="Scottish Gaelic">Gaelic</a> term referring to an "enclosure" (<span title="Scottish Gaelic-language text"><i lang="gd">lann</i></span>),<sup id="cite_ref-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-9">&#91;A&#93;</a></sup> but it could less plausibly be from the Anglo-Saxon term "folkland" (land held by <a href="/wiki/Anglo-Saxon_law#Folk-right_and_privilege" title="Anglo-Saxon law">folk-right</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoom2006129_10-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoom2006129-10">&#91;9&#93;</a></sup> The name "Falklands" was not applied to the islands until 1765, when <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Great_Britain" title="Kingdom of Great Britain">British</a> captain <a href="/wiki/John_Byron" title="John Byron">John Byron</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Navy" title="Royal Navy">Royal Navy</a> claimed them for <a href="/wiki/George_III_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="George III of the United Kingdom">King George III</a> as "Falkland's Islands".<sup id="cite_ref-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The term "Falklands" is a standard abbreviation used to refer to the islands.
</p><p>The common Spanish name for the archipelago, <i>Islas Malvinas</i>, derives from the French <i>Îles Malouines</i>—the name given to the islands by <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_France" title="Kingdom of France">French</a> explorer <a href="/wiki/Louis-Antoine_de_Bougainville" class="mw-redirect" title="Louis-Antoine de Bougainville">Louis-Antoine de Bougainville</a> in 1764.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHince2001121_12-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHince2001121-12">&#91;11&#93;</a></sup> Bougainville, who founded the islands' first settlement, named the area after the port of <a href="/wiki/Saint-Malo" title="Saint-Malo">Saint-Malo</a> (the point of departure for his ships and colonists).<sup id="cite_ref-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-13">&#91;12&#93;</a></sup> The port, located in the <a href="/wiki/Brittany" title="Brittany">Brittany</a> region of western France, was named after <a href="/wiki/Malo_(saint)" title="Malo (saint)">St. Malo</a> (or Maclou), the Christian <a href="/wiki/Evangelism" title="Evangelism">evangelist</a> who founded the city.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBalmaceda2011Chapter_36_14-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBalmaceda2011Chapter_36-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1965, at the 20th session<sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup> of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly" title="United Nations General Assembly">United Nations General Assembly</a>, the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Fourth_Committee" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Fourth Committee">Fourth Committee</a> determined that, in all languages other than Spanish, all UN documentation would designate the territory as <i>Falkland Islands (Malvinas)</i>. In Spanish the territory was designated as <i>Islas Malvinas (Falkland Islands)</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEForeign_Office196180_16-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEForeign_Office196180-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The nomenclature used by the United Nations for statistical processing purposes is <i>Falkland Islands (Malvinas)</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-std_name_17-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-std_name-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="History of the Falkland Islands">History of the Falkland Islands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Timeline_of_the_history_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Timeline of the history of the Falkland Islands">Timeline of the history of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<p>Although <a href="/wiki/Fuegians" title="Fuegians">Fuegians</a> from <a href="/wiki/Patagonia" title="Patagonia">Patagonia</a> may have visited the Falkland Islands in prehistoric times,<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> the islands were uninhabited when Europeans first explored them.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECarafano2005367_20-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarafano2005367-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> European claims of discovery date back to the 16th century, but no consensus exists on whether early explorers sighted the Falklands or other islands in the South Atlantic.<sup id="cite_ref-M_White_21-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-M_White-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGoebel1971xiv–xv_22-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGoebel1971xiv–xv-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;B&#93;</a></sup> The first undisputed landing on the islands is attributed to English captain John Strong, who, en route to Peru and Chile's littoral in 1690, explored the Falkland Sound and noted the islands' water and game.<sup id="cite_ref-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-25">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Falklands remained uninhabited until the 1764 establishment of <a href="/wiki/Port_Louis,_Falkland_Islands" title="Port Louis, Falkland Islands">Port Louis</a> on <a href="/wiki/East_Falkland" title="East Falkland">East Falkland</a> by French captain <a href="/wiki/Louis_Antoine_de_Bougainville" title="Louis Antoine de Bougainville">Louis Antoine de Bougainville</a> and the 1766 foundation of <a href="/wiki/Port_Egmont" title="Port Egmont">Port Egmont</a> on <a href="/wiki/Saunders_Island,_Falkland_Islands" title="Saunders Island, Falkland Islands">Saunders Island</a> by British captain <a href="/wiki/John_MacBride_(Royal_Navy_officer)" title="John MacBride (Royal Navy officer)">John MacBride</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;C&#93;</a></sup> Whether or not the settlements were aware of each other's existence is debated by historians.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> In 1766, France surrendered its claim on the Falklands to Spain, which renamed the French colony <a href="/wiki/Puerto_Soledad" title="Puerto Soledad">Puerto Soledad</a> the following year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESegal1991240_30-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESegal1991240-30">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Problems began when Spain detected and <a href="/wiki/Capture_of_Port_Egmont" title="Capture of Port Egmont">captured Port Egmont</a> in 1770. <a href="/wiki/Falklands_Crisis_(1770)" class="mw-redirect" title="Falklands Crisis (1770)">War</a> was narrowly avoided by its restitution to Britain in 1771.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199826_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199826-31">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The British and Spanish settlements coexisted in the archipelago until 1774, when Britain's new economic and strategic considerations led it to voluntarily withdraw from the islands, leaving a plaque claiming the Falklands for King George III.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199826–27_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199826–27-32">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> Spain's <a href="/wiki/Viceroyalty_of_the_R%C3%ADo_de_la_Plata" title="Viceroyalty of the Río de la Plata">Viceroyalty of the Río de la Plata</a> became the only governmental presence in the territory. <a href="/wiki/West_Falkland" title="West Falkland">West Falkland</a> was left abandoned, and Puerto Soledad became mostly a prison camp.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199827_33-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199827-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Amid the <a href="/wiki/British_invasions_of_the_R%C3%ADo_de_la_Plata" class="mw-redirect" title="British invasions of the Río de la Plata">British invasions of the Río de la Plata</a> during the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a> in Europe, the islands' governor evacuated the archipelago in 1806; Spain's remaining colonial garrison followed suit in 1811, except for <a href="/wiki/Gaucho" title="Gaucho">gauchos</a> and fishermen who remained voluntarily.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199827_33-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199827-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Thereafter the archipelago was visited only by fishing ships; its political status was undisputed until 1820, when Colonel <a href="/wiki/David_Jewett" title="David Jewett">David Jewett</a>, an American <a href="/wiki/Privateer" title="Privateer">privateer</a> working for the <a href="/wiki/United_Provinces_of_the_R%C3%ADo_de_la_Plata" title="United Provinces of the Río de la Plata">United Provinces of the Río de la Plata</a>, informed anchored ships about <a href="/wiki/Buenos_Aires" title="Buenos Aires">Buenos Aires</a>' 1816 claim to Spain's territories in the South Atlantic.<sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;D&#93;</a></sup> Since the islands had no permanent inhabitants, in 1823 Buenos Aires granted German-born merchant <a href="/wiki/Luis_Vernet" title="Luis Vernet">Luis Vernet</a> permission to conduct fishing activities and exploit feral cattle in the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;E&#93;</a></sup> Vernet settled at the ruins of Puerto Soledad in 1826, and accumulated resources on the islands until the venture was secure enough to bring settlers and form a permanent colony.<sup id="cite_ref-40" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-40">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> Buenos Aires named Vernet military and civil commander of the islands in 1829,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPascoePepper2008540–46_41-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPascoePepper2008540–46-41">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> and he attempted to regulate sealing to stop the activities of foreign whalers and sealers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199827_33-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199827-33">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> Vernet's venture lasted until a dispute over fishing and hunting rights led to <a href="/wiki/Falklands_Expedition" title="Falklands Expedition">a raid</a> by the <a href="/wiki/United_States_Ship" title="United States Ship">American warship</a> <a href="/wiki/USS_Lexington_(1825)" title="USS Lexington (1825)">USS <i>Lexington</i></a> in 1831,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPascoePepper2008541–44_42-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPascoePepper2008541–44-42">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;F&#93;</a></sup> when <a href="/wiki/United_States_Navy" title="United States Navy">United States Navy</a> commander <a href="/wiki/Silas_Duncan" title="Silas Duncan">Silas Duncan</a> declared the dissolution of the island's government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEPeterson1964106_44-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEPeterson1964106-44">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe,_Mount_William,_Falkland_Islands,_May_1849_(Portion_B).png" class="image"><img alt="Three men in horseback examine a pastoral settlement" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe%2C_Mount_William%2C_Falkland_Islands%2C_May_1849_%28Portion_B%29.png/220px-Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe%2C_Mount_William%2C_Falkland_Islands%2C_May_1849_%28Portion_B%29.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="84" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe%2C_Mount_William%2C_Falkland_Islands%2C_May_1849_%28Portion_B%29.png/330px-Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe%2C_Mount_William%2C_Falkland_Islands%2C_May_1849_%28Portion_B%29.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe%2C_Mount_William%2C_Falkland_Islands%2C_May_1849_%28Portion_B%29.png/440px-Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe%2C_Mount_William%2C_Falkland_Islands%2C_May_1849_%28Portion_B%29.png 2x" data-file-width="605" data-file-height="230" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Edward_Gennys_Fanshawe,_Mount_William,_Falkland_Islands,_May_1849_(Portion_B).png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Depiction of a Falklands <a href="/wiki/Corral" class="mw-redirect" title="Corral">corral</a>, shepherds and sheep in 1849 (painting by Royal Navy Admiral <a href="/wiki/Edward_Fanshawe" title="Edward Fanshawe">Edward Fanshawe</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>Buenos Aires attempted to retain influence over the settlement by installing a garrison, but a mutiny in 1832 was followed the next year by the arrival of British forces who <a href="/wiki/Reassertion_of_British_sovereignty_over_the_Falkland_Islands_(1833)" title="Reassertion of British sovereignty over the Falkland Islands (1833)">reasserted Britain's rule</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGraham-Yooll200250_45-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham-Yooll200250-45">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Argentine_Confederation" title="Argentine Confederation">Argentine Confederation</a> (headed by Buenos Aires Governor <a href="/wiki/Juan_Manuel_de_Rosas" title="Juan Manuel de Rosas">Juan Manuel de Rosas</a>) protested against Britain's actions,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot198325–26_46-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot198325–26-46">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-49" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-49">&#91;G&#93;</a></sup> and Argentine governments have continued since then to register official protests against Britain.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGustafson198834–35_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGustafson198834–35-50">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-52" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-52">&#91;H&#93;</a></sup> The British troops departed after completing their mission, leaving the area without formal government.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGraham-Yooll200251–52_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham-Yooll200251–52-53">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> Vernet's deputy, the Scotsman <a href="/wiki/Matthew_Brisbane" title="Matthew Brisbane">Matthew Brisbane</a>, returned to the islands that year to restore the business, but his efforts ended after, amid unrest at Port Louis, gaucho <a href="/wiki/Antonio_Rivero" title="Antonio Rivero">Antonio Rivero</a> led a group of dissatisfied individuals to murder Brisbane and the settlement's senior leaders; survivors hid in a cave on a nearby island until the British returned and restored order.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGraham-Yooll200251–52_53-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGraham-Yooll200251–52-53">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup> In 1840, the Falklands became a <a href="/wiki/Crown_colony" title="Crown colony">Crown colony</a> and Scottish settlers subsequently established an official pastoral community.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAldrichConnell1998201_54-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAldrichConnell1998201-54">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> Four years later, nearly everyone relocated to Port Jackson, considered a better location for government, and merchant <a href="/wiki/Samuel_Fisher_Lafone" title="Samuel Fisher Lafone">Samuel Lafone</a> began a venture to encourage British colonisation.<sup id="cite_ref-55" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-55">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Stanley,_Falkland_Islands" title="Stanley, Falkland Islands">Stanley</a>, as Port Jackson was soon renamed, officially became the seat of government in 1845.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot19839_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot19839-56">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Early in its history Stanley had a negative reputation due to cargo-shipping losses; only in emergencies would ships rounding <a href="/wiki/Cape_Horn" title="Cape Horn">Cape Horn</a> stop at the port.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBernhardson2011Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History_57-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBernhardson2011Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History-57">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Nevertheless the Falklands' geographic location proved ideal for ship repairs and the "Wrecking Trade", the business of selling and buying shipwrecks and their cargoes.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStrange198772–74_58-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStrange198772–74-58">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Aside from this trade, commercial interest in the archipelago was minimal due to the low-value hides of the feral cattle roaming the pastures. Economic growth began only after the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_Holdings" class="mw-redirect" title="Falkland Islands Holdings">Falkland Islands Company</a>, which bought out Lafone's failing enterprise in 1851,<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;I&#93;</a></sup> successfully introduced <a href="/wiki/Cheviot_sheep" title="Cheviot sheep">Cheviot sheep</a> for wool farming, spurring other farms to follow suit.<sup id="cite_ref-Bernhardson_2011_loc=Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History_61-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bernhardson_2011_loc=Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History-61">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup> The high cost of importing materials, combined with the shortage of labour and consequent high wages, meant the ship repair trade became uncompetitive. After 1870 it declined as the replacement of sail ships by <a href="/wiki/Steamship" title="Steamship">steamships</a> was accelerated by the low cost of coal in South America; by 1914, with the opening of the <a href="/wiki/Panama_Canal" title="Panama Canal">Panama Canal</a>, the trade effectively ended.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEStrange198772–73_62-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEStrange198772–73-62">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> In 1881, the Falkland Islands became financially independent of Britain.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot19839_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot19839-56">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> For more than a century, the Falkland Islands Company dominated the trade and employment of the archipelago; in addition, it owned most housing in Stanley, which greatly benefited from the wool trade with the UK.<sup id="cite_ref-Bernhardson_2011_loc=Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History_61-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bernhardson_2011_loc=Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History-61">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands,_1914_(retouched).jpg" class="image"><img alt="Two battling ships, with one sinking" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands%2C_1914_%28retouched%29.jpg/220px-Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands%2C_1914_%28retouched%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="133" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands%2C_1914_%28retouched%29.jpg/330px-Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands%2C_1914_%28retouched%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands%2C_1914_%28retouched%29.jpg/440px-Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands%2C_1914_%28retouched%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="800" data-file-height="482" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands,_1914_(retouched).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Naval confrontation during the 1914 <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Battle of the Falkland Islands">Battle of the Falkland Islands</a> (painting by <a href="/wiki/William_Lionel_Wyllie" title="William Lionel Wyllie">William Lionel Wyllie</a>)</div></div></div>
<p>In the first half of the 20th century the Falklands served an important role in Britain's territorial claims to <a href="/wiki/Subantarctic" title="Subantarctic">subantarctic</a> islands and a section of Antarctica. The Falklands governed these territories as the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_Dependencies" title="Falkland Islands Dependencies">Falkland Islands Dependencies</a> starting in 1908, and retained them until their dissolution in 1985.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEDay2013129–30_63-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEDay2013129–30-63">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup> The Falklands also played a minor role in the two world wars as a military base aiding control of the South Atlantic. In the <a href="/wiki/First_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="First World War">First World War</a> <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Battle of the Falkland Islands">Battle of the Falkland Islands</a> in December 1914 a Royal Navy fleet defeated an <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">Imperial German</a> squadron. In the <a href="/wiki/Second_World_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Second World War">Second World War</a>, following the December 1939 <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_the_River_Plate" title="Battle of the River Plate">Battle of the River Plate</a>, the battle-damaged <a href="/wiki/HMS_Exeter_(68)" title="HMS Exeter (68)">HMS <i>Exeter</i></a> steamed to the Falklands for repairs.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECarafano2005367_20-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECarafano2005367-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> In 1942, a battalion en route to India was redeployed to the Falklands as a garrison amid fears of a Japanese seizure of the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHaddelseyCarroll2014Prologue_64-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHaddelseyCarroll2014Prologue-64">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> After the war ended, the Falklands economy was affected by declining wool prices and the political uncertainty resulting from the revived sovereignty dispute between the United Kingdom and Argentina.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBernhardson2011Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History_57-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBernhardson2011Stanley_and_Vicinity:_History-57">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Simmering tensions between the UK and Argentina increased during the second half of the century, when Argentine President <a href="/wiki/Juan_Per%C3%B3n" title="Juan Perón">Juan Perón</a> asserted sovereignty over the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEZepeda2005102_65-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEZepeda2005102-65">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> The sovereignty dispute intensified during the 1960s, shortly after the United Nations passed a resolution on <a href="/wiki/Decolonisation" class="mw-redirect" title="Decolonisation">decolonisation</a> which Argentina interpreted as favourable to its position.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELaver2001125_66-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELaver2001125-66">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> In 1965, the UN General Assembly passed <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_General_Assembly_Resolution_2065" title="United Nations General Assembly Resolution 2065">Resolution 2065</a>, calling for both states to conduct bilateral negotiations to reach a peaceful settlement of the dispute.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELaver2001125_66-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELaver2001125-66">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> From 1966 until 1968, the UK confidentially discussed with Argentina the transfer of the Falklands, assuming its judgement would be accepted by the islanders.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas199124_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas199124-67">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> An agreement on trade ties between the archipelago and the mainland was reached in 1971 and, consequently, Argentina built a temporary airfield at Stanley in 1972.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot19839_56-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEReginaldElliot19839-56">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> Nonetheless, Falklander dissent, as expressed by their strong lobby in the <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Parliament of the United Kingdom">UK Parliament</a>, and tensions between the UK and Argentina effectively limited sovereignty negotiations until 1977.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas199124–27_68-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas199124–27-68">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Concerned at the expense of maintaining the Falkland Islands in an era of budget cuts, the UK again considered transferring sovereignty to Argentina in the early <a href="/wiki/Premiership_of_Margaret_Thatcher" title="Premiership of Margaret Thatcher">Thatcher government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Guardian_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Guardian-69">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> Substantive sovereignty talks again ended by 1981, and the dispute escalated with passing time.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEThomas199128–31_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEThomas199128–31-70">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> In April 1982 the <a href="/wiki/Falklands_War" title="Falklands War">Falklands War</a> began when Argentine military forces <a href="/wiki/1982_invasion_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="1982 invasion of the Falkland Islands">invaded the Falklands</a> and other <a href="/wiki/South_Georgia_and_the_South_Sandwich_Islands" title="South Georgia and the South Sandwich Islands">British territories in the South Atlantic</a>, briefly <a href="/wiki/Occupation_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Occupation of the Falkland Islands">occupying them</a> until a UK <a href="/wiki/Expeditionary_warfare" title="Expeditionary warfare">expeditionary force</a> retook the territories in June.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup> After the war the UK expanded its military presence, building <a href="/wiki/RAF_Mount_Pleasant" title="RAF Mount Pleasant">RAF Mount Pleasant</a> and increasing the size of its garrison.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran1998130–35_72-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran1998130–35-72">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup> The war also left some 117 minefields containing nearly 20,000 mines of various types, including anti-vehicle and anti-personnel mines.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_Mines_73-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_Mines-73">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Due to the large number of <a href="/wiki/Demining" title="Demining">deminer</a> casualties, initial attempts to clear the mines ceased in 1983.<sup id="cite_ref-BBC_Mines_73-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBC_Mines-73">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;J&#93;</a></sup> Demining operations recommenced in 2009 and were completed in October 2020.<sup id="cite_ref-PenguinNews_76-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-PenguinNews-76">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Based on <a href="/wiki/Edward_Shackleton,_Baron_Shackleton" title="Edward Shackleton, Baron Shackleton">Lord Shackleton</a>'s recommendations, the Falklands diversified from a sheep-based monoculture into an economy of tourism and, with the establishment of the Falklands <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a>, fisheries.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECawkell2001147_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECawkell2001147-77">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;K&#93;</a></sup> The road network was also made more extensive, and the construction of RAF Mount Pleasant allowed access to <a href="/wiki/Flight_length" title="Flight length">long haul</a> flights.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECawkell2001147_77-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECawkell2001147-77">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Oil exploration also began in the 2010s, with indications of possible commercially exploitable deposits in the Falklands basin.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> Landmine clearance work restarted in 2009, in accordance with the UK's obligations under the <a href="/wiki/Ottawa_Treaty" title="Ottawa Treaty">Ottawa Treaty</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sapper_Hill" title="Sapper Hill">Sapper Hill</a> Corral was cleared of mines in 2012, allowing access to an important historical landmark for the first time in 30 years.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Argentina and the UK re-established diplomatic relations in 1990, but neither has agreed on the terms of future sovereignty discussions.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government">Government</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Politics of the Falkland Islands">Politics of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Government_House,_Falkland_Islands.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Large, rambling house with greenhouse and white fence" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Government_House%2C_Falkland_Islands.jpg/220px-Government_House%2C_Falkland_Islands.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="124" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Government_House%2C_Falkland_Islands.jpg/330px-Government_House%2C_Falkland_Islands.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9d/Government_House%2C_Falkland_Islands.jpg/440px-Government_House%2C_Falkland_Islands.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1280" data-file-height="720" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Government_House,_Falkland_Islands.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Government_House,_Falkland_Islands" title="Government House, Falkland Islands">Government House</a> in <a href="/wiki/Stanley,_Falkland_Islands" title="Stanley, Falkland Islands">Stanley</a> is the Governor's <a href="/wiki/Official_residence" title="Official residence">official residence</a>.</div></div></div>
<p>The Falkland Islands are a <a href="/wiki/Self-governing_colony" title="Self-governing colony">self-governing</a> <a href="/wiki/British_Overseas_Territory" class="mw-redirect" title="British Overseas Territory">British Overseas Territory</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECahill2010&quot;Falkland_Islands&quot;_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECahill2010&quot;Falkland_Islands&quot;-83">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> Under the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_Constitution" class="mw-redirect" title="Falkland Islands Constitution">2009 Constitution</a>, the islands have full internal self-government; the UK is responsible for foreign affairs, retaining the power "to protect UK interests and to ensure the overall good governance of the territory".<sup id="cite_ref-Constitution_84-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Constitution-84">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Monarchy_of_the_United_Kingdom" title="Monarchy of the United Kingdom">Monarch of the United Kingdom</a> is the head of state, and executive authority is exercised on the monarch's behalf by the <a href="/wiki/Governor_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Governor of the Falkland Islands">governor</a>, who appoints the islands' <a href="/wiki/Chief_Executive_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Chief Executive of the Falkland Islands">chief executive</a> on the advice of members of the <a href="/wiki/Legislative_Assembly_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Legislative Assembly of the Falkland Islands">Legislative Assembly</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-constitutiontext_85-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitutiontext-85">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> Both the governor and the chief executive serve as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBuckman2012394_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBuckman2012394-86">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Governor <a href="/wiki/Alison_Blake" title="Alison Blake">Alison Blake</a> was appointed in July 2022<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> and Chief Executive Andy Keeling was appointed in April 2021.<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> The UK minister responsible for the Falkland Islands since 2022, <a href="/wiki/Zac_Goldsmith" title="Zac Goldsmith">Zac Goldsmith</a>, administers British foreign policy regarding the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The governor acts on the advice of the islands' <a href="/wiki/Executive_Council_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Executive Council of the Falkland Islands">Executive Council</a>, composed of the chief executive, the <a href="/wiki/Director_of_Finance_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Director of Finance of the Falkland Islands">Director of Finance</a> and three elected members of the Legislative Assembly (with the governor as chairman).<sup id="cite_ref-constitutiontext_85-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitutiontext-85">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The Legislative Assembly, a <a href="/wiki/Unicameralism" title="Unicameralism">unicameral legislature</a>, consists of the chief executive, the director of finance and eight members (five from Stanley and three from <a href="/wiki/Camp_(Falkland_Islands)" title="Camp (Falkland Islands)">Camp</a>) elected to four-year terms by <a href="/wiki/Universal_suffrage" title="Universal suffrage">universal suffrage</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-constitutiontext_85-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-constitutiontext-85">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> All politicians in the Falkland Islands are <a href="/wiki/Independent_(politician)" class="mw-redirect" title="Independent (politician)">independent</a>; no political parties exist on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Government&quot;_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Government&quot;-90">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Since the <a href="/wiki/2013_Falkland_Islands_general_election" title="2013 Falkland Islands general election">2013 general election</a>, members of the Legislative Assembly have received a salary and are expected to work full-time and give up all previously held jobs or business interests.<sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>As a territory of the United Kingdom, the Falklands were part of the <a href="/wiki/Special_member_state_territories_and_the_European_Union" class="mw-redirect" title="Special member state territories and the European Union">overseas countries and territories</a> of the European Union until <a href="/wiki/Brexit" title="Brexit">2020</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup> The islands' judicial system, overseen by the <a href="/wiki/Foreign_and_Commonwealth_Office" class="mw-redirect" title="Foreign and Commonwealth Office">Foreign and Commonwealth Office</a>, is largely based on <a href="/wiki/English_law" title="English law">English law</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESainato2010157–158_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESainato2010157–158-93">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> and the constitution binds the territory to the principles of the <a href="/wiki/European_Convention_on_Human_Rights" title="European Convention on Human Rights">European Convention on Human Rights</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Constitution_84-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Constitution-84">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Residents have the right of appeal to the <a href="/wiki/European_Court_of_Human_Rights" title="European Court of Human Rights">European Court of Human Rights</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Privy_Council_of_the_United_Kingdom" class="mw-redirect" title="Privy Council of the United Kingdom">Privy Council</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-94" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-94">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> Law enforcement is the responsibility of the <a href="/wiki/Royal_Falkland_Islands_Police" title="Royal Falkland Islands Police">Royal Falkland Islands Police</a> (RFIP).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESainato2010157–158_93-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESainato2010157–158-93">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> 
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Defence">Defence</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Military_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Military of the Falkland Islands">Military of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<p>Defence of the islands is provided by the United Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Transportation&quot;_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Transportation&quot;-96">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> A British military garrison is stationed on the islands, and the Falkland Islands government funds an additional <a href="/wiki/Platoon_(military_unit)" class="mw-redirect" title="Platoon (military unit)">platoon</a> to <a href="/wiki/Company_(military_unit)" title="Company (military unit)">company</a>-sized <a href="/wiki/Light_infantry" title="Light infantry">light infantry</a> <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_Defence_Force" title="Falkland Islands Defence Force">Falkland Islands Defence Force</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-timesfidf_97-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-timesfidf-97">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> The Falklands claim an <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> (EEZ) extending 200&#160;nmi (370&#160;km) from its coastal baselines, based on the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Convention_on_the_Law_of_the_Sea" title="United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea">United Nations Convention on the Law of the Sea</a>; this zone overlaps with the EEZ of Argentina.<sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Sovereignty_dispute">Sovereignty dispute</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_dispute" title="Falkland Islands sovereignty dispute">Falkland Islands sovereignty dispute</a></div>
<p>The UK and Argentina both assert sovereignty over the Falkland Islands. The UK bases its position on its continuous administration of the islands since 1833 and the islanders' "right to self-determination as set out in the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Charter" class="mw-redirect" title="United Nations Charter">UN Charter</a>".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELansford20121528_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELansford20121528-99">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Argentina claims that, when it <a href="/wiki/Argentine_Declaration_of_Independence" title="Argentine Declaration of Independence">achieved independence</a> in 1816, it acquired the Falklands from Spain.<sup id="cite_ref-RIS_102-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RIS-102">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto1_103-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto1-103">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_104-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-104">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Reassertion_of_British_sovereignty_over_the_Falkland_Islands_(1833)" title="Reassertion of British sovereignty over the Falkland Islands (1833)">incident of 1833</a> is particularly contentious; Argentina considers it proof of "Britain's usurpation" whereas the UK discounts it as a mere reassertion of its claim.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGustafson198826–27_105-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGustafson198826–27-105">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-108" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-108">&#91;L&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2009 the British prime minister, <a href="/wiki/Gordon_Brown" title="Gordon Brown">Gordon Brown</a>, had a meeting with the Argentine president, <a href="/wiki/Cristina_Fern%C3%A1ndez_de_Kirchner" title="Cristina Fernández de Kirchner">Cristina Fernández de Kirchner</a>, and said that there would be no further talks over the sovereignty of the Falklands.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> In March 2013 the Falkland Islands held a <a href="/wiki/2013_Falkland_Islands_sovereignty_referendum" title="2013 Falkland Islands sovereignty referendum">referendum</a> on its political status: 99.8% of votes cast favoured remaining a British overseas territory.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> Argentina does not recognise the Falkland Islanders as a partner in negotiations.<sup id="cite_ref-RIS_102-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-RIS-102">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Geography of the Falkland Islands">Geography of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg" class="image"><img alt="Topographic image" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg/220px-Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="172" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg/330px-Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4e/Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg/440px-Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="2550" data-file-height="1990" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Falkland_Islands_topographic_map-en.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Map of the Falkland Islands</div></div></div>
<p>The Falkland Islands have a land area of 4,700&#160;sq&#160;mi (12,000&#160;km<sup>2</sup>) and a coastline estimated at 800&#160;mi (1,300&#160;km).<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The archipelago consists of two main islands, West Falkland and East Falkland, and 776 smaller islands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTESainato2010157_115-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTESainato2010157-115">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup> The islands are predominantly mountainous and hilly,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Geography&quot;_116-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Geography&quot;-116">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> with the major exception being the depressed plains of <a href="/wiki/Lafonia" title="Lafonia">Lafonia</a> (a peninsula forming the southern part of East Falkland).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTETrewby200279_117-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTETrewby200279-117">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The Falklands consists of <a href="/wiki/Continental_crust" title="Continental crust">continental crust</a> fragments resulting from the break-up of <a href="/wiki/Gondwana" title="Gondwana">Gondwana</a> and the opening of the South Atlantic that began 130&#160;million years ago. The islands are located in the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">South Atlantic Ocean</a>, on the <a href="/wiki/Patagonian_Shelf" title="Patagonian Shelf">Patagonian Shelf</a>, about 300&#160;mi (480&#160;km) east of Patagonia in southern Argentina.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlügel200966_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlügel200966-118">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Falklands' approximate location is latitude <span class="nowrap">51°40′</span>&#160;– <span class="nowrap">53°00′ S</span> and longitude <span class="nowrap">57°40′</span>&#160;– <span class="nowrap">62°00′ W</span>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuo2007112_119-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuo2007112-119">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> The archipelago's two main islands are separated by the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Sound" title="Falkland Sound">Falkland Sound</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHemmerle2005318_120-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHemmerle2005318-120">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> and its deep coastal indentations form <a href="/wiki/Natural_harbour" class="mw-redirect" title="Natural harbour">natural harbours</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> East Falkland houses Stanley (the capital and largest settlement),<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGuo2007112_119-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGuo2007112-119">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> the UK military base at RAF Mount Pleasant, and the archipelago's highest point: <a href="/wiki/Mount_Usborne" title="Mount Usborne">Mount Usborne</a>, at 2,313&#160;ft (705&#160;m).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHemmerle2005318_120-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHemmerle2005318-120">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> Outside of these significant settlements is the area colloquially known as "Camp", which is derived from the Spanish term for countryside (<i>Campo</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHince2001&quot;Camp&quot;_122-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHince2001&quot;Camp&quot;-122">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Climate of the Falkland Islands">climate of the islands</a> is cold, windy, and humid <a href="/wiki/Oceanic_climate" title="Oceanic climate">maritime</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEKlügel200966_118-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEKlügel200966-118">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> Variability of daily weather is typical throughout the archipelago.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199816_123-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199816-123">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Rainfall is common over half of the year, averaging 610&#160;mm (24&#160;in) in Stanley, and sporadic light snowfall occurs nearly all year.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Geography&quot;_116-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Geography&quot;-116">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> The temperature has historically stayed between 21.1 and −11.1&#160;°C (70.0 and 12.0&#160;°F) in Stanley, with mean monthly temperatures varying from 9&#160;°C (48&#160;°F) early in the year to −1&#160;°C (30&#160;°F) in July.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199816_123-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199816-123">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Strong <a href="/wiki/Westerlies" title="Westerlies">westerly winds</a> and cloudy skies are common.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Geography&quot;_116-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTECentral_Intelligence_Agency2011&quot;Falkland_Islands_(Malvinas)&amp;nbsp;–_Geography&quot;-116">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup> Although numerous storms are recorded each month, conditions are normally calm.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEGibran199816_123-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEGibran199816-123">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Wildlife_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Wildlife of the Falkland Islands">Wildlife of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Necksaundersisland2.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Large group of short, squat penguins on barren shore" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Necksaundersisland2.jpg/220px-Necksaundersisland2.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Necksaundersisland2.jpg/330px-Necksaundersisland2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c6/Necksaundersisland2.jpg/440px-Necksaundersisland2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2048" data-file-height="1361" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Necksaundersisland2.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Colony of <a href="/wiki/Southern_rockhopper_penguin" title="Southern rockhopper penguin">southern rockhopper penguins</a> on <a href="/wiki/Saunders_Island,_Falkland_Islands" title="Saunders Island, Falkland Islands">Saunders Island</a></div></div></div>
<p>The Falkland Islands are <a href="/wiki/Biogeography" title="Biogeography">biogeographically</a> part of the <a href="/wiki/Antarctic_ecozone" class="mw-redirect" title="Antarctic ecozone">Antarctic zone</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJónsdóttir200784–86_124-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJónsdóttir200784–86-124">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> with strong connections to the flora and fauna of <a href="/wiki/Patagonia" title="Patagonia">Patagonia</a> in mainland South America.<sup id="cite_ref-epk_125-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-epk-125">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup> Land birds make up most of the Falklands' <a href="/wiki/Bird" title="Bird">avifauna</a>; 63 species breed on the islands, including 16 <a href="/wiki/Endemic" class="mw-redirect" title="Endemic">endemic</a> species.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985131_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985131-126">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> There is also abundant <a href="/wiki/Arthropod" title="Arthropod">arthropod</a> diversity on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985132_127-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985132-127">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> The Falklands' flora consists of 163 native <a href="/wiki/Vascular_plant" title="Vascular plant">vascular species</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985129_128-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985129-128">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> More than 400 species of <a href="/wiki/Lichen" title="Lichen">lichens</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lichenicolous_fungus" title="Lichenicolous fungus">lichen-dwelling fungi</a> have been recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-Fryday_et_al._2019_129-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Fryday_et_al._2019-129">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> The islands' only native terrestrial mammal, the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_wolf" title="Falkland Islands wolf">warrah</a>, was hunted to extinction by European settlers.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHince2001370_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHince2001370-130">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The islands are frequented by <a href="/wiki/Marine_mammal" title="Marine mammal">marine mammals</a>, such as the <a href="/wiki/Southern_elephant_seal" title="Southern elephant seal">southern elephant seal</a> and the <a href="/wiki/South_American_fur_seal" title="South American fur seal">South American fur seal</a>, and various types of <a href="/wiki/Cetacea" title="Cetacea">cetaceans</a>; offshore islands house the rare <a href="/wiki/Striated_caracara" title="Striated caracara">striated caracara</a>. There are also five different penguin species and a few of the largest <a href="/wiki/Albatross" title="Albatross">albatross</a> colonies on the planet.<sup id="cite_ref-Science_and_Diplomacy._131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Science_and_Diplomacy.-131">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> Endemic fish around the islands are primarily from the genus <i><a href="/wiki/Galaxias" title="Galaxias">Galaxias</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985132_127-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEClarkDingwall1985132-127">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> The Falklands are treeless and have a wind-resistant vegetation predominantly composed of a variety of <a href="/wiki/Subshrub" title="Subshrub">dwarf shrubs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEJónsdóttir200785_132-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEJónsdóttir200785-132">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Virtually the entire land area of the islands is used as pasture for sheep.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_133-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-133">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Introduced species include <a href="/wiki/Reindeer" title="Reindeer">reindeer</a>, hares, rabbits, <a href="/wiki/South_American_gray_fox" title="South American gray fox">Patagonian foxes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Brown_rat" title="Brown rat">brown rats</a>, and cats.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBell2007544_134-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBell2007544-134">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup> Several of these species have harmed native flora and fauna, so the government has tried to contain, remove or exterminate foxes, rabbits and rats. Endemic land animals have been the most affected by introduced species, and several bird species have been extirpated from the larger islands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBell2007542–545_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBell2007542–545-135">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> The extent of <a href="/wiki/Human_impact_on_the_environment" title="Human impact on the environment">human impact</a> on the Falklands is unclear, since there is little long-term data on habitat change.<sup id="cite_ref-epk_125-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-epk-125">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Economy of the Falkland Islands">Economy of the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Aerial photograph of small seaside city" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg/220px-Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg/330px-Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d2/Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg/440px-Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Aerial_photo_Port_Stanley_edit.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Stanley,_Falkland_Islands" title="Stanley, Falkland Islands">Stanley</a> is the financial centre of the Falkland Islands' economy.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoyle2001171_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoyle2001171-136">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup></div></div></div>
<p>The economy of the Falkland Islands is ranked the 222nd largest out of 229 in the world by GDP (<a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">PPP</a>), but ranks 5th worldwide by <a href="/wiki/GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" class="mw-redirect" title="GDP (PPP) per capita">GDP (PPP) per capita</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-137" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-137">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> The unemployment rate was 1% in 2016, and inflation was calculated at 1.4% in 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_133-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-133">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup> Based on 2010 data, the islands have a high <a href="/wiki/Human_Development_Index" title="Human Development Index">Human Development Index</a> of 0.874<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAvakov201347_4-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvakov201347-4">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> and a moderate <a href="/wiki/Gini_coefficient" title="Gini coefficient">Gini coefficient</a> for <a href="/wiki/Economic_inequality" title="Economic inequality">income inequality</a> of 34.17.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEAvakov201354_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEAvakov201354-138">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> The local currency is the <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islands_pound" title="Falkland Islands pound">Falkland Islands pound</a>, which is <a href="/wiki/Fixed_exchange-rate_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Fixed exchange-rate system">pegged</a> to the British <a href="/wiki/Pound_sterling" title="Pound sterling">pound sterling</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Economic development was advanced by <a href="/wiki/Shipyard" title="Shipyard">ship resupplying</a> and sheep farming for high-quality wool.<sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The main sheep breeds in the Falkland Islands are <a href="/wiki/Polwarth_(sheep)" class="mw-redirect" title="Polwarth (sheep)">Polwarth</a> and <a href="/wiki/Corriedale" title="Corriedale">Corriedale</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> During the 1980s, although ranch under-investment and the use of <a href="/wiki/Synthetic_fibre" class="mw-redirect" title="Synthetic fibre">synthetic fibres</a> damaged the sheep-farming sector, the government secured a major revenue stream by the establishment of an <a href="/wiki/Exclusive_economic_zone" title="Exclusive economic zone">exclusive economic zone</a> and the sale of fishing licences to "anybody wishing to fish within this zone".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoyle2001170_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoyle2001170-142">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Since the end of the Falklands War in 1982, the islands' economic activity increasingly focused on oil field <a href="/wiki/Hydrocarbon_exploration" title="Hydrocarbon exploration">exploration</a> and tourism.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEHemmerle2005319_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEHemmerle2005319-143">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup> All large settlements are now connected by road and, since 2008, a ferry links West and East Falkland.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_144-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-144">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> The islands' major exports include wool, hides, venison, fish and squid; its main imports include fuel, <a href="/wiki/Building_material" title="Building material">building materials</a> and clothing.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_133-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA-133">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The port settlement of Stanley has regained the islands' economic focus, with an increase in population as workers migrate from Camp.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoyle2001170–171_145-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoyle2001170–171-145">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> Fear of dependence on fishing licences and threats from <a href="/wiki/Overfishing" title="Overfishing">overfishing</a>, <a href="/wiki/Illegal_fishing" class="mw-redirect" title="Illegal fishing">illegal fishing</a> and fish <a href="/wiki/Market_trend" title="Market trend">market price fluctuations</a> led to increased interest in oil drilling as an alternative source of revenue; as of 2001 exploration efforts had yet to find "exploitable reserves".<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoyle2001171_136-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoyle2001171-136">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup> Development projects in education and sports have been funded by the Falklands government, without aid from the United Kingdom.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoyle2001170_142-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoyle2001170-142">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Primary_sector_of_the_economy" title="Primary sector of the economy">primary sector of the economy</a> accounts for most of the Falkland Islands' gross domestic product, with the fishing industry alone contributing between 50% and 60% of annual GDP; agriculture also contributes significantly to GDP and employs about a tenth of the population.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> A little over a quarter of the workforce serves the Falkland Islands government, making it the archipelago's largest employer.<sup id="cite_ref-FI_Guardian_147-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FI_Guardian-147">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Tourism, part of the service economy, has been spurred by increased interest in <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Antarctica" title="Tourism in Antarctica">Antarctic exploration</a> and the creation of direct air links with the United Kingdom and South America.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup> Tourists, mostly <a href="/wiki/Cruise_ship" title="Cruise ship">cruise ship</a> passengers, are attracted by the archipelago's wildlife and environment, as well as activities such as fishing and <a href="/wiki/Wreck_diving" title="Wreck diving">wreck diving</a>; the majority find accommodation in Stanley.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup> The main international airport, located at <a href="/wiki/RAF_Mount_Pleasant" title="RAF Mount Pleasant">RAF Mount Pleasant</a> on East Falkland, provides flights to <a href="/wiki/RAF_Brize_Norton" title="RAF Brize Norton">RAF Brize Norton</a> in the UK and mainland South America.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica.com_144-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica.com-144">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Port_Stanley_Airport" title="Port Stanley Airport">Port Stanley Airport</a> provides internal flights.<sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup> Despite <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_the_Falkland_Islands" title="COVID-19 pandemic in the Falkland Islands">COVID-19 pandemic</a> restrictions causing suspensions of flights from <a href="/wiki/Santiago" title="Santiago">Santiago</a> and <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A3o_Paulo" title="São Paulo">São Paulo</a> and prohibited cruise ship tourism, the economy of the islands remains stable and healthy.<sup id="cite_ref-151" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-151">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Origins_of_Falkland_Islanders" title="Origins of Falkland Islanders">Origins of Falkland Islanders</a> and <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Religion in the Falkland Islands">Religion in the Falkland Islands</a></div>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:FAL-2016-Stanley,_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg" class="image"><img alt="Photograph of a building" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/FAL-2016-Stanley%2C_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg/220px-FAL-2016-Stanley%2C_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/FAL-2016-Stanley%2C_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg/330px-FAL-2016-Stanley%2C_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/05/FAL-2016-Stanley%2C_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg/440px-FAL-2016-Stanley%2C_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg 2x" data-file-width="8694" data-file-height="6532" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:FAL-2016-Stanley,_Falkland_Islands%E2%80%93Christ_Church_Cathedral.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Christ_Church_Cathedral_(Falkland_Islands)" title="Christ Church Cathedral (Falkland Islands)">Christ Church Cathedral</a>, the local parish church of the <a href="/wiki/Anglican_Communion" title="Anglican Communion">Anglican Communion</a>. Most Falklanders identify themselves as Christian.</div></div></div>
<p>The Falkland Islands population is homogeneous, mostly descended from Scottish and Welsh immigrants who settled in the territory after 1833.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELaver20019_152-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELaver20019-152">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup> The Falkland-born population are also descended from English and <a href="/wiki/French_people" title="French people">French people</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gibraltarian_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Gibraltarian people">Gibraltarians</a>, Scandinavians, and South Americans. The 2016 census indicated that 43% of residents were born on the archipelago, with foreign-born residents assimilated into local culture. The legal term for the right of residence is "belonging to the islands".<sup id="cite_ref-2006Census_153-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2006Census-153">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Census_2016_154-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census_2016-154">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup> In 1983, full <a href="/wiki/British_citizenship" class="mw-redirect" title="British citizenship">British citizenship</a> was given to Falkland Islanders under the <a href="/wiki/British_Nationality_(Falkland_Islands)_Act_1983" title="British Nationality (Falkland Islands) Act 1983">British Nationality (Falkland Islands) Act</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTELaver20019_152-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTELaver20019-152">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>A significant population decline affected the archipelago in the 20th century, with many young islanders moving overseas in search of education, a modern lifestyle, and better job opportunities,<sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup> particularly to the British city of <a href="/wiki/Southampton" title="Southampton">Southampton</a>, which came to be known in the islands as "Stanley North".<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> In recent years, the islands' population decline has reduced, thanks to immigrants from the United Kingdom, <a href="/wiki/Saint_Helena" title="Saint Helena">Saint Helena</a>, and Chile.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEMinahan2013139_157-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEMinahan2013139-157">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup> In the 2012 census, a majority of residents listed their nationality as <a href="/wiki/Falkland_Islander" class="mw-redirect" title="Falkland Islander">Falkland Islander</a> (59 per cent), followed by British (29 per cent), Saint Helenian (9.8 per cent), and <a href="/wiki/Chileans_in_the_Falkland_Islands" title="Chileans in the Falkland Islands">Chilean</a> (5.4 per cent).<sup id="cite_ref-census2012_158-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census2012-158">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> A small number of <a href="/wiki/Argentine_people" class="mw-redirect" title="Argentine people">Argentines</a> also live on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-mercopress_159-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-mercopress-159">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Falkland Islands <a href="/wiki/List_of_South_American_countries_by_population" title="List of South American countries by population">have a low population density</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERoyle2006181_160-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERoyle2006181-160">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2012 census, the average daily population of the Falklands was 2,932, excluding military personnel serving in the archipelago and their dependents.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;M&#93;</a></sup> A 2012 report counted 1,300 uniformed personnel and 50 <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Defence_(United_Kingdom)" title="Ministry of Defence (United Kingdom)">British Ministry of Defence</a> civil servants present in the Falklands.<sup id="cite_ref-FI_Guardian_147-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FI_Guardian-147">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Stanley (with 2,121 residents) is the most-populous location on the archipelago, followed by <a href="/wiki/RAF_Mount_Pleasant" title="RAF Mount Pleasant">Mount Pleasant</a> (369 residents, primarily air-base contractors) and Camp (351 residents).<sup id="cite_ref-census2012_158-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-census2012-158">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> The islands' age distribution is skewed towards working age <span class="nowrap">(20–60)</span>. Males outnumber females (53 to 47 per cent), and this discrepancy is most prominent in the <span class="nowrap">20–60</span> age group.<sup id="cite_ref-2006Census_153-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2006Census-153">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 2012 census, most islanders identified themselves as Christian (66 per cent), followed by those with no religious affiliation (32 per cent).
